# Plan
## Plan
1. Create a new ESP-IDF project targeting the ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1 and add the `led_strip` component dependency.
2. Configure the onboard RGB LED on GPIO8 using the RMT-backed `led_strip` driver, initially keeping it off and then driving the blue channel.
3. Configure the BOOT button on GPIO9 as an active-low input with an internal pull-up. Use polling with a 50 ms debounce window so each press generates one event, including correct handling of held buttons and bounce.
4. Add a FreeRTOS queue between the button task and blink task. Start at 1 Hz, increase by 1 Hz per valid press, and cap the frequency at 10 Hz. Use a 50% duty cycle and a blue intensity of 64/255 to avoid excessive brightness.
5. Build, flash, and monitor the firmware. Verify that the LED blinks at the initial rate, each released-and-pressed button cycle increases the rate, bouncing does not cause multiple increments, and the rate remains capped at 10 Hz.

Defaults: NimBLE/Wi-Fi are not involved; no provisioning or credentials are needed. GPIO8 and GPIO9 are used exactly as defined by the board context, and no restricted flash-connected GPIOs are assigned.

**Alternatives:** If the managed `led_strip` dependency or its API does not match the installed ESP-IDF version, pin the compatible component version and adapt the RMT initialization while retaining GPIO8 and the same pixel API. If the LED appears to use a different color order or brightness convention, verify the DevKitM-1 LED wiring and adjust only the color format/channel values. If button presses are unreliable, verify the active-low GPIO9 wiring and increase the debounce interval modestly rather than adding an interrupt prone to bounce.

✓ Found 1 source(s) for 'led_strip.h' — use these exact signatures.

// ===== managed_components/espressif__led_strip/include/led_strip.h =====
/*
 * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2022-2024 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
 *
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
 */
#pragma once

#include <stdint.h>
#include "esp_err.h"
#include "led_strip_rmt.h"
#include "led_strip_spi.h"

#ifdef __cplusplus
extern "C" {
#endif

/**
 * @brief Set RGB for a specific pixel
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 * @param index: index of pixel to set
 * @param red: red part of color
 * @param green: green part of color
 * @param blue: blue part of color
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Set RGB for a specific pixel successfully
 *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: Set RGB for a specific pixel failed because of invalid parameters
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Set RGB for a specific pixel failed because other error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_set_pixel(led_strip_handle_t strip, uint32_t index, uint32_t red, uint32_t green, uint32_t blue);

/**
 * @brief Set RGBW for a specific pixel
 *
 * @note Only call this function if your led strip does have the white component (e.g. SK6812-RGBW)
 * @note Also see `led_strip_set_pixel` if you only want to specify the RGB part of the color and bypass the white component
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 * @param index: index of pixel to set
 * @param red: red part of color
 * @param green: green part of color
 * @param blue: blue part of color
 * @param white: separate white component
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Set RGBW color for a specific pixel successfully
 *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: Set RGBW color for a specific pixel failed because of an invalid argument
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Set RGBW color for a specific pixel failed because other error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_set_pixel_rgbw(led_strip_handle_t strip, uint32_t index, uint32_t red, uint32_t green, uint32_t blue, uint32_t white);

/**
 * @brief Set HSV for a specific pixel
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 * @param index: index of pixel to set
 * @param hue: hue part of color (0 - 360)
 * @param saturation: saturation part of color (0 - 255, rescaled from 0 - 1. e.g. saturation = 0.5, rescaled to 127)
 * @param value: value part of color (0 - 255, rescaled from 0 - 1. e.g. value = 0.5, rescaled to 127)
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Set HSV color for a specific pixel successfully
 *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: Set HSV color for a specific pixel failed because of an invalid argument
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Set HSV color for a specific pixel failed because other error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_set_pixel_hsv(led_strip_handle_t strip, uint32_t index, uint16_t hue, uint8_t saturation, uint8_t value);

/**
 * @brief Set HSV for a specific pixel in 16-bit resolution
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 * @param index: index of pixel to set
 * @param hue: hue part of color (0 - 360)
 * @param saturation: saturation part of color (0 - 65535, rescaled from 0 - 1. e.g. saturation = 0.5, rescaled to 32767)
 * @param value: value part of color (0 - 65535, rescaled from 0 - 1. e.g. value = 0.5, rescaled to 32767)
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Set HSV color for a specific pixel successfully
 *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: Set HSV color for a specific pixel failed because of an invalid argument
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Set HSV color for a specific pixel failed because other error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_set_pixel_hsv_16(led_strip_handle_t strip, uint32_t index, uint16_t hue, uint16_t saturation, uint16_t value);

/**
 * @brief Refresh memory colors to LEDs
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Refresh successfully
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Refresh failed because some other error occurred
 *
 * @note:
 *      After updating the LED colors in the memory, a following invocation of this API is needed to flush colors to strip.
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_refresh(led_strip_handle_t strip);

/**
 * @brief Clear LED strip (turn off all LEDs)
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Clear LEDs successfully
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Clear LEDs failed because some other error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_clear(led_strip_handle_t strip);

/**
 * @brief Free LED strip resources
 *
 * @param strip: LED strip
 *
 * @return
 *      - ESP_OK: Free resources successfully
 *      - ESP_FAIL: Free resources failed because error occurred
 */
esp_err_t led_strip_del(led_strip_handle_t strip);

#ifdef __cplusplus
}
#endif

// ===== CMakeLists.txt =====
# The following five lines of boilerplate have to be in your project's
# CMakeLists in this exact order for cmake to work correctly
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.16)

include($ENV{IDF_PATH}/tools/cmake/project.cmake)
# "Trim" the build. Include the minimal set of components, main, and anything it depends on.
idf_build_set_property(MINIMAL_BUILD ON)
project(blink)


// ===== sdkconfig.defaults =====
CONFIG_BLINK_LED_GPIO=y
CONFIG_BLINK_GPIO=8


// ===== main/CMakeLists.txt =====
idf_component_register(SRCS "blink_example_main.c"
                       INCLUDE_DIRS ".")


// ===== main/Kconfig.projbuild =====
menu "Example Configuration"

    orsource "$IDF_PATH/examples/common_components/env_caps/$IDF_TARGET/Kconfig.env_caps"

    choice BLINK_LED
        prompt "Blink LED type"
        default BLINK_LED_GPIO
        help
            Select the LED type. A normal level controlled LED or an addressable LED strip.
            The default selection is based on the Espressif DevKit boards.
            You can change the default selection according to your board.

        config BLINK_LED_GPIO
            bool "GPIO"
        config BLINK_LED_STRIP
            bool "LED strip"
    endchoice

    choice BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND
        depends on BLINK_LED_STRIP
        prompt "LED strip backend peripheral"
        default BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_RMT if SOC_RMT_SUPPORTED
        default BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_SPI
        help
            Select the backend peripheral to drive the LED strip.

        config BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_RMT
            depends on SOC_RMT_SUPPORTED
            bool "RMT"
        config BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_SPI
            bool "SPI"
    endchoice

    config BLINK_GPIO
        int "Blink GPIO number"
        range ENV_GPIO_RANGE_MIN ENV_GPIO_OUT_RANGE_MAX
        default 8
        help
            GPIO number (IOxx) to blink on and off the LED.
            Some GPIOs are used for other purposes (flash connections, etc.) and cannot be used to blink.

    config BLINK_PERIOD
        int "Blink period in ms"
        range 10 3600000
        default 1000
        help
            Define the blinking period in milliseconds.

endmenu


// ===== main/blink_example_main.c =====
/* Blink Example

   This example code is in the Public Domain (or CC0 licensed, at your option.)

   Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, this
   software is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR
   CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
*/
#include <stdio.h>
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "driver/gpio.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "led_strip.h"
#include "sdkconfig.h"

static const char *TAG = "example";

/* Use project configuration menu (idf.py menuconfig) to choose the GPIO to blink,
   or you can edit the following line and set a number here.
*/
#define BLINK_GPIO CONFIG_BLINK_GPIO

static uint8_t s_led_state = 0;

#ifdef CONFIG_BLINK_LED_STRIP

static led_strip_handle_t led_strip;

static void blink_led(void)
{
    /* If the addressable LED is enabled */
    if (s_led_state) {
        /* Set the LED pixel using RGB from 0 (0%) to 255 (100%) for each color */
        led_strip_set_pixel(led_strip, 0, 16, 16, 16);
        /* Refresh the strip to send data */
        led_strip_refresh(led_strip);
    } else {
        /* Set all LED off to clear all pixels */
        led_strip_clear(led_strip);
    }
}

static void configure_led(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Example configured to blink addressable LED!");
    /* LED strip initialization with the GPIO and pixels number*/
    led_strip_config_t strip_config = {
        .strip_gpio_num = BLINK_GPIO,
        .max_leds = 1, // at least one LED on board
    };
#if CONFIG_BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_RMT
    led_strip_rmt_config_t rmt_config = {
        .resolution_hz = 10 * 1000 * 1000, // 10MHz
        .flags.with_dma = false,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(led_strip_new_rmt_device(&strip_config, &rmt_config, &led_strip));
#elif CONFIG_BLINK_LED_STRIP_BACKEND_SPI
    led_strip_spi_config_t spi_config = {
        .spi_bus = SPI2_HOST,
        .flags.with_dma = true,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(led_strip_new_spi_device(&strip_config, &spi_config, &led_strip));
#else
#error "unsupported LED strip backend"
#endif
    /* Set all LED off to clear all pixels */
    led_strip_clear(led_strip);
}

#elif CONFIG_BLINK_LED_GPIO

static void blink_led(void)
{
    /* Set the GPIO level according to the state (LOW or HIGH)*/
    gpio_set_level(BLINK_GPIO, s_led_state);
}

static void configure_led(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Example configured to blink GPIO LED!");
    gpio_reset_pin(BLINK_GPIO);
    /* Set the GPIO as a push/pull output */
    gpio_set_direction(BLINK_GPIO, GPIO_MODE_OUTPUT);
}

#else
#error "unsupported LED type"
#endif

void app_main(void)
{

    /* Configure the peripheral according to the LED type */
    configure_led();

    while (1) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Turning the LED %s!", s_led_state == true ? "ON" : "OFF");
        blink_led();
        /* Toggle the LED state */
        s_led_state = !s_led_state;
        vTaskDelay(CONFIG_BLINK_PERIOD / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
    }
}


// ===== main/idf_component.yml =====
dependencies:
  espressif/led_strip: "^3.0.0"

// ===== managed_components/espressif__led_strip/include/led_strip_rmt.h =====
    1  /*
    2   * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2022-2024 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
    3   *
    4   * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
    5   */
    6  #pragma once
    7  
    8  #include <stdint.h>
    9  #include "esp_err.h"
   10  #include "led_strip_types.h"
   11  #include "esp_idf_version.h"
   12  #include "driver/rmt_types.h"
   13  
   14  #ifdef __cplusplus
   15  extern "C" {
   16  #endif
   17  
   18  /**
   19   * @brief LED Strip RMT specific configuration
   20   */
   21  typedef struct {
   22      rmt_clock_source_t clk_src; /*!< RMT clock source */
   23      uint32_t resolution_hz;     /*!< RMT tick resolution, if set to zero, a default resolution (10MHz) will be applied */
   24      size_t mem_block_symbols;   /*!< How many RMT symbols can one RMT channel hold at one time. Set to 0 will fallback to use the default size. */
   25      /*!< Extra RMT specific driver flags */
   26      struct led_strip_rmt_extra_config {
   27          uint32_t with_dma: 1;   /*!< Use DMA to transmit data */
   28      } flags;                    /*!< Extra driver flags */
   29  } led_strip_rmt_config_t;
   30  
   31  /**
   32   * @brief Create LED strip based on RMT TX channel
   33   *
   34   * @param led_config LED strip configuration
   35   * @param rmt_config RMT specific configuration
   36   * @param ret_strip Returned LED strip handle
   37   * @return
   38   *      - ESP_OK: create LED strip handle successfully
   39   *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: create LED strip handle failed because of invalid argument
   40   *      - ESP_ERR_NO_MEM: create LED strip handle failed because of out of memory
   41   *      - ESP_FAIL: create LED strip handle failed because some other error
   42   */
   43  esp_err_t led_strip_new_rmt_device(const led_strip_config_t *led_config, const led_strip_rmt_config_t *rmt_config, led_strip_handle_t *ret_strip);
   44  
   45  #ifdef __cplusplus
   46  }
   47  #endif

📄 edit: \\?\C:\Users\TALWA\OneDrive\Desktop\firmgen\projects\esp32_test\my_firmware\blinky_button\firmware\services\blinky_button_service.c (L1–1)
+ #include "blinky_button_service.h"
+ #include "app_config.h"
+ #include "logger.h"
+ #include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
+ #include "freertos/queue.h"
+ #include "freertos/task.h"
+ #include "driver/gpio.h"
+ #include "esp_err.h"
+ #include "led_strip.h"
+ #include "led_strip_rmt.h"
+ static const char *TAG = "blinky";
+ static led_strip_handle_t s_led_strip;
+ static QueueHandle_t s_press_queue;
+ static int led_init(void)
+ {
+  const led_strip_config_t strip_config = {
+  .strip_gpio_num = APP_LED_GPIO,
+  .max_leds = 1,
+  };
+  const led_strip_rmt_config_t rmt_config = {
+  .resolution_hz = 10 * 1000 * 1000,
+  .flags.with_dma = false,
+  };
+  esp_err_t err = led_strip_new_rmt_device(&strip_config, &rmt_config, &s_led_strip);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "LED initialization failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  return -1;
+  }
+  err = led_strip_clear(s_led_strip);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "LED clear failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  led_strip_del(s_led_strip);
+  s_led_strip = NULL;
+  return -1;
+  }
+  return 0;
+ }
+ static int button_init(void)
+ {
+  const gpio_config_t config = {
+  .pin_bit_mask = 1ULL << APP_BUTTON_GPIO,
+  .mode = GPIO_MODE_INPUT,
+  .pull_up_en = GPIO_PULLUP_ENABLE,
+  .pull_down_en = GPIO_PULLDOWN_DISABLE,
+  .intr_type = GPIO_INTR_DISABLE,
+  };
+  esp_err_t err = gpio_config(&config);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "button initialization failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  return -1;
+  }
+  return 0;
+ }
+ static void button_task(void *arg)
+ {
+  (void)arg;
+  int last_sample = 1;
+  int stable_state = 1;
+  int stable_ms = 0;
+  for (;;) {
+  int sample = gpio_get_level(APP_BUTTON_GPIO);
+  if (sample == last_sample) {
+  if (stable_ms < APP_BUTTON_DEBOUNCE_MS) {
+  stable_ms += APP_BUTTON_SAMPLE_MS;
+  }
+  } else {
+  last_sample = sample;
+  stable_ms = 0;
+  }
+  if (stable_ms >= APP_BUTTON_DEBOUNCE_MS && sample != stable_state) {
+  stable_state = sample;
+  if (stable_state == 0) {
+  uint8_t press = 1;
+  if (xQueueSend(s_press_queue, &press, 0) != pdPASS) {
+  ESP_LOGW(TAG, "press queue full; ignoring button press");
+  }
+  }
+  }
+  vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(APP_BUTTON_SAMPLE_MS));
+  }
+ }
+ static void blink_task(void *arg)
+ {
+  (void)arg;
+  int frequency_hz = APP_INITIAL_FREQUENCY_HZ;
+  bool led_on = false;
+  uint8_t press;
+  for (;;) {
+  while (xQueueReceive(s_press_queue, &press, 0) == pdPASS) {
+  if (frequency_hz < APP_MAX_FREQUENCY_HZ) {
+  frequency_hz++;
+  }
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "button press accepted; blink frequency is %d Hz", frequency_hz);
+  }
+  led_on = !led_on;
+  if (led_on) {
+  led_strip_set_pixel(s_led_strip, 0, 0, 0, APP_LED_BLUE_LEVEL);
+  led_strip_refresh(s_led_strip);
+  } else {
+  led_strip_clear(s_led_strip);
+  }
+  vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000 / (2 * frequency_hz)));
+  }
+ }
+ int blinky_button_service_start(void)
+ {
+  if (led_init() != 0 || button_init() != 0) {
+  return -1;
+  }
+  s_press_queue = xQueueCreate(APP_EVENT_QUEUE_LENGTH, sizeof(uint8_t));
+  if (s_press_queue == NULL) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "failed to create press queue");
+  led_strip_clear(s_led_strip);
+  return -1;
+  }
+  if (xTaskCreate(button_task, "button_task", 3072, NULL, 5, NULL) != pdPASS ||
+  xTaskCreate(blink_task, "blink_task", 4096, NULL, 5, NULL) != pdPASS) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "failed to create button/blink task");
+  return -1;
+  }
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "started at %d Hz; press BOOT to increase up to %d Hz", APP_INITIAL_FREQUENCY_HZ, APP_MAX_FREQUENCY_HZ);
+  return 0;
+ }

Implemented the blinky/button firmware.

Behavior:

  • Blue onboard RGB LED: GPIO8
  • BOOT button: GPIO9, active-low with internal pull-up
  • Starts blinking at 1 Hz
  • Each debounced button press increases the frequency by 1 Hz
  • Maximum frequency: 10 Hz
  • Blue brightness limited to 64/255
  • Button handling uses a FreeRTOS task and bounded queue
  • Presses are logged over serial

📝 Code changes (1 chunk)
📄 edit: \\?\C:\Users\TALWA\OneDrive\Desktop\firmgen\projects\esp32_test\my_firmware\blinky_button\firmware\docs\overview.md (L1–1)
+ # ESP32-C3 Blinky Button Firmware
+ This firmware blinks the ESP32-C3 DevKitM-1 onboard RGB LED blue. It starts at 1 Hz and increases the blink frequency by 1 Hz for each debounced BOOT-button press, up to 10 Hz.
+ ## Hardware map
+ - Onboard RGB LED data: GPIO8
+ - Onboard BOOT button: GPIO9, active-low with the internal pull-up enabled
+ - LED output: blue channel at brightness 64/255
+ - Blink duty cycle: 50%
+ ## Build and flash
+ The project targets ESP32-C3 and uses the ESP-IDF `espressif/led_strip` component with the RMT backend. Build and flash using the ESP-IDF project tooling, then monitor at 115200 baud. Startup and accepted button presses are logged with the `app` and `blinky` tags.
+ ## Modules
+ - `firmware/app/app.c`: application startup orchestration.
+ - `firmware/services/blinky_button_service.c`: LED initialization, debounced button polling, press queue, and blink-frequency state machine.
+ - `firmware/configs/app_config.h`: GPIO assignments and application constants.
+ - `firmware/utils/logger/logger.h`: ESP-IDF logging include wrapper.

The firmware is running without reported crashes or boot loops. The monitor confirmed one valid BOOT-button press and the frequency increase from 1 Hz to 2 Hz. Please visually confirm that the onboard LED is blinking blue and becomes faster when you press the button.

blinky_button/firmware/docs/api/html/index.html

U
okay we have to integrate mp6050 with our esp32 how much wires we need
AI

For a typical MPU6050 breakout module, you need 4 wires for basic I²C operation:

| MPU6050 | ESP32-C3 | |---|---| | VCC | 3.3 V | | GND | GND | | SDA | GPIO4 | | SCL | GPIO5 |

The ESP32-C3 board context has GPIO4 and GPIO5 available and safe for I²C.

Optional wires

  • INT → any available GPIO, if you want motion/data-ready interrupts. This is not required for normal polling.
  • AD0 → GND for I²C address 0x68 (usually already connected on the module).
  • AD0 → 3.3 V changes the address to 0x69.

So:

  • Basic connection: 4 wires
  • With interrupt support: 5 wires
  • With manually connected address selection: potentially 6 wires

Use 3.3 V logic. Do not connect raw MPU6050 logic signals directly to 5 V. Many breakout boards include voltage regulation and level shifting, but 3.3 V is the safest supply for the ESP32-C3 setup.

U
on mpu side do i need to connect on vcc_in or 3.3v ?
AI

Use the MPU6050 board’s 3.3V or VCC pin and connect it to the ESP32-C3’s 3V3 pin.

  • MPU6050 3.3V/VCC → ESP32 3V3
  • MPU6050 GND → ESP32 GND
  • Do not connect both VCC_IN and 3.3V simultaneously.

VCC_IN is board-specific. On some breakout boards it is the input to an onboard regulator and may accept 5 V; on others it is intended for a particular supply range. Unless the module documentation explicitly says otherwise, use the clearly labeled 3.3 V pin.

If your module only has VCC_IN and no 3.3V pin, send a photo or the exact board name so the correct voltage can be confirmed.

U
okay give me connections to make on esp32 side ?
AI

Connect the MPU6050 to the ESP32-C3 as follows:

| MPU6050 pin | ESP32-C3 connection | |---|---| | 3.3V or VCC | 3V3 | | GND | GND | | SDA | GPIO4 | | SCL | GPIO5 |

Optional connections:

| MPU6050 pin | Connection | |---|---| | INT | Leave unconnected for polling, or connect to GPIO6 for interrupt support | | AD0 | GND for address 0x68; leave it as the module provides unless changing the address |

Use the following basic wiring:

MPU6050 3.3V  → ESP32-C3 3V3
MPU6050 GND   → ESP32-C3 GND
MPU6050 SDA   → ESP32-C3 GPIO4
MPU6050 SCL   → ESP32-C3 GPIO5

The current blinky firmware uses GPIO8 for the LED and GPIO9 for the BOOT button, so GPIO4 and GPIO5 are available for I²C. Ensure the MPU6050 module has I²C pull-up resistors; many breakout boards include them.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Treat the requested “I2S scanner” as an I2C scanner, since the MPU6050 is wired over I2C. Use GPIO4 for SDA and GPIO5 for SCL, with 3.3 V logic and no INT connection by default.
2. Update the ESP-IDF component requirements and application configuration for the ESP32-C3 I2C master driver, scanner timing, MPU6050 addresses, and logging interval. Keep all existing LED/button GPIO assignments unchanged.
3. Create a temporary I2C scanner service that probes the valid 7-bit address range and clearly reports devices at 0x68 or 0x69. The scanner will use a conservative 100 kHz bus speed, bounded probe timeouts, and error logging.
4. Temporarily replace the blinky application startup with the scanner so the scanner can be flashed without changing the final LED/button behavior. Leave the LED off during scanner mode.
5. Build, flash, and monitor the scanner on COM5. Confirm whether the MPU6050 responds, record its address, and check for bus errors or unexpected responding devices.
6. After scanner evidence is available, proceed only if an MPU6050 address is detected; otherwise stop the sensor integration path and troubleshoot power, ground, SDA/SCL wiring, pull-ups, and AD0 before retrying.
7. Create the MPU6050 service using the installed ESP-IDF I2C master API. Auto-detect 0x68/0x69, verify WHO_AM_I, wake the device, retain default ±2 g and ±250 °/s ranges, and periodically read the 14-byte accelerometer/temperature/gyroscope register block.
8. Restore the existing blinky/button service and add the MPU6050 logger to normal application startup. Log converted acceleration, temperature, and gyroscope values at 10 Hz while keeping sensor failures non-fatal to the LED/button service.
9. Build, flash, and monitor the final firmware. Verify stable readings at rest, changing readings when the board is moved, correct handling of both possible I2C addresses, continued LED/button operation, and absence of repeated I2C errors. Update the project README and overview documentation with the wiring and runtime behavior.

Defaults: scanner mode temporarily replaces the application only for hardware validation; the final firmware restores the blue LED/button behavior and adds sensor logging. The sensor uses polling rather than interrupts, 100 kHz I2C, a 100 ms logging period, runtime probing of both MPU6050 addresses, and bounded retries. No restricted GPIO11–17 pins will be used.

**Alternatives:** If the installed ESP-IDF does not provide the modern I2C master API, use the version-matching legacy driver consistently and update CMake requirements accordingly. If no address responds, verify that MPU6050 VCC/VCC_IN is correctly powered at the module-supported voltage, grounds are common, SDA/SCL are not swapped, pull-up resistors are present, and AD0 selects 0x68 or 0x69. If WHO_AM_I or data reads fail despite a detected address, reduce the bus speed to 50 kHz, increase the transaction timeout modestly, and retry initialization; do not assign GPIO11–17 or add an interrupt pin unless a later hardware decision requires it.

   16  #endif

   19   * @brief I2C master bus specific configurations
   20   */
   21  typedef struct {
   22      i2c_port_num_t i2c_port;              /*!< I2C port number, `-1` for auto selecting, (not include LP I2C instance) */
   23      gpio_num_t sda_io_num;                /*!< GPIO number of I2C SDA signal, pulled-up internally */
   24      gpio_num_t scl_io_num;                /*!< GPIO number of I2C SCL signal, pulled-up internally */
   25      union {
   26          i2c_clock_source_t clk_source;        /*!< Clock source of I2C master bus */
   27  #if SOC_LP_I2C_SUPPORTED
   28          lp_i2c_clock_source_t lp_source_clk;       /*!< LP_UART source clock selection */
   29  #endif
   30      };
   31      uint8_t glitch_ignore_cnt;            /*!< If the glitch period on the line is less than this value, it can be filtered out, typically value is 7 (unit: I2C module clock cycle)*/
   32      int intr_priority;                    /*!< I2C interrupt priority, if set to 0, driver will select the default priority (1,2,3). */
   33      size_t trans_queue_depth;             /*!< Depth of internal transfer queue, increase this value can support more transfers pending in the background, only valid in asynchronous transaction. (Typically max_device_num * per_transaction)*/
   34      struct {
   35          uint32_t enable_internal_pullup: 1;  /*!< Enable internal pullups. Note: This is not strong enough to pullup buses under high-speed frequency. Recommend proper external pull-up if possible */
   36          uint32_t allow_pd:               1;  /*!< If set, the driver will backup/restore the I2C registers before/after entering/exist sleep mode.
   37                                                By this approach, the system can power off I2C's power domain.
   38                                                This can save power, but at the expense of more RAM being consumed */
   39      } flags;                              /*!< I2C master config flags */
   40  } i2c_master_bus_config_t;
   41  
   42  #define I2C_DEVICE_ADDRESS_NOT_USED    (0xffff) /*!< Skip carry address bit in driver transmit and receive */
   43  
   44  /**
   45   * @brief I2C device configuration
   46   */
   47  typedef struct {
   48      i2c_addr_bit_len_t dev_addr_length;         /*!< Select the address length of the slave device. */
   49      uint16_t device_address;                    /*!< I2C device raw address. (The 7/10 bit address without read/write bit). Macro I2C_DEVICE_ADDRESS_NOT_USED (0xFFFF) stands for skip the address config inside driver. */
   50      uint32_t scl_speed_hz;                      /*!< I2C SCL line frequency. */
   51      uint32_t scl_wait_us;                      /*!< Timeout value. (unit: us). Please note this value should not be so small that it can handle stretch/disturbance properly. If 0 is set, that means use the default reg value*/
   52      struct {
   53          uint32_t disable_ack_check:      1;     /*!< Disable ACK check. If this is set false, that means ack check is enabled, the transaction will be stopped and API returns error when nack is detected. */
   54      } flags;                                    /*!< I2C device config flags */
   55  } i2c_device_config_t;

// ===== main/i2c_basic_example_main.c =====
/*
 * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2024 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
 *
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Unlicense OR CC0-1.0
 */
/* i2c - Simple Example

   Simple I2C example that shows how to initialize I2C
   as well as reading and writing from and to registers for a sensor connected over I2C.

   The sensor used in this example is a MPU9250 inertial measurement unit.
*/
#include <stdio.h>
#include "sdkconfig.h"
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "driver/i2c_master.h"

static const char *TAG = "example";

#define I2C_MASTER_SCL_IO           CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SCL       /*!< GPIO number used for I2C master clock */
#define I2C_MASTER_SDA_IO           CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SDA       /*!< GPIO number used for I2C master data  */
#define I2C_MASTER_NUM              I2C_NUM_0                   /*!< I2C port number for master dev */
#define I2C_MASTER_FREQ_HZ          CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_FREQUENCY /*!< I2C master clock frequency */
#define I2C_MASTER_TX_BUF_DISABLE   0                           /*!< I2C master doesn't need buffer */
#define I2C_MASTER_RX_BUF_DISABLE   0                           /*!< I2C master doesn't need buffer */
#define I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS       1000

#define MPU9250_SENSOR_ADDR         0x68        /*!< Address of the MPU9250 sensor */
#define MPU9250_WHO_AM_I_REG_ADDR   0x75        /*!< Register addresses of the "who am I" register */
#define MPU9250_PWR_MGMT_1_REG_ADDR 0x6B        /*!< Register addresses of the power management register */
#define MPU9250_RESET_BIT           7

/**
 * @brief Read a sequence of bytes from a MPU9250 sensor registers
 */
static esp_err_t mpu9250_register_read(i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle, uint8_t reg_addr, uint8_t *data, size_t len)
{
    return i2c_master_transmit_receive(dev_handle, &reg_addr, 1, data, len, I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
}

/**
 * @brief Write a byte to a MPU9250 sensor register
 */
static esp_err_t mpu9250_register_write_byte(i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle, uint8_t reg_addr, uint8_t data)
{
    uint8_t write_buf[2] = {reg_addr, data};
    return i2c_master_transmit(dev_handle, write_buf, sizeof(write_buf), I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
}

/**
 * @brief i2c master initialization
 */
static void i2c_master_init(i2c_master_bus_handle_t *bus_handle, i2c_master_dev_handle_t *dev_handle)
{
    i2c_master_bus_config_t bus_config = {
        .i2c_port = I2C_MASTER_NUM,
        .sda_io_num = I2C_MASTER_SDA_IO,
        .scl_io_num = I2C_MASTER_SCL_IO,
        .clk_source = I2C_CLK_SRC_DEFAULT,
        .glitch_ignore_cnt = 7,
        .flags.enable_internal_pullup = true,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_config, bus_handle));

    i2c_device_config_t dev_config = {
        .dev_addr_length = I2C_ADDR_BIT_LEN_7,
        .device_address = MPU9250_SENSOR_ADDR,
        .scl_speed_hz = I2C_MASTER_FREQ_HZ,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_master_bus_add_device(*bus_handle, &dev_config, dev_handle));
}

void app_main(void)
{
    uint8_t data[2];
    i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus_handle;
    i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle;
    i2c_master_init(&bus_handle, &dev_handle);
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "I2C initialized successfully");

    /* Read the MPU9250 WHO_AM_I register, on power up the register should have the value 0x71 */
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(mpu9250_register_read(dev_handle, MPU9250_WHO_AM_I_REG_ADDR, data, 1));
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "WHO_AM_I = %X", data[0]);

    /* Demonstrate writing by resetting the MPU9250 */
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(mpu9250_register_write_byte(dev_handle, MPU9250_PWR_MGMT_1_REG_ADDR, 1 << MPU9250_RESET_BIT));

    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_master_bus_rm_device(dev_handle));
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_del_master_bus(bus_handle));
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "I2C de-initialized successfully");
}

// ===== C:/Espressif/esp-idf-release-v5.5/components/esp_driver_i2c/include/driver/i2c_master.h =====
  223   *      - ESP_OK: I2C master receive success
  224   *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: I2C master receive parameter invalid.
  225   *      - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Operation timeout(larger than xfer_timeout_ms) because the bus is busy or hardware crash.
  226   */
  227  esp_err_t i2c_master_receive(i2c_master_dev_handle_t i2c_dev, uint8_t *read_buffer, size_t read_size, int xfer_timeout_ms);
  228  
  229  /**
  230   * @brief Probe I2C address, if address is correct and ACK is received, this function will return ESP_OK.
  231   *
  232   * @param[in] bus_handle I2C master device handle that created by `i2c_master_bus_add_device`.
  233   * @param[in] address I2C device address that you want to probe.
  234   * @param[in] xfer_timeout_ms Wait timeout, in ms. Note: -1 means wait forever (Not recommended in this function).
  235   *
  236   * @attention Pull-ups must be connected to the SCL and SDA pins when this function is called. If you get `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT
  237   * while `xfer_timeout_ms` was parsed correctly, you should check the pull-up resistors. If you do not have proper resistors nearby.
  238   * `flags.enable_internal_pullup` is also acceptable.
  239   *
  240   * @note The principle of this function is to sent device address with a write command. If the device on your I2C bus, there would be an ACK signal and function
  241   * returns `ESP_OK`. If the device is not on your I2C bus, there would be a NACK signal and function returns `ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND`. `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT` is not an expected
  242   * failure, which indicated that the i2c probe not works properly, usually caused by pull-up resistors not be connected properly. Suggestion check data on SDA/SCL line
  243   * to see whether there is ACK/NACK signal is on line when i2c probe function fails.
  244   *
  245   * @note There are lots of I2C devices all over the world, we assume that not all I2C device support the behavior like `device_address+nack/ack`.
  246   * So, if the on line data is strange and no ack/nack got respond. Please check the device datasheet.
  247   *
  248   * @return
  249   *      - ESP_OK: I2C device probe successfully
  250   *      - ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND: I2C probe failed, doesn't find the device with specific address you gave.
  251   *      - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Operation timeout(larger than xfer_timeout_ms) because the bus is busy or hardware crash.
  252   */
  253  esp_err_t i2c_master_probe(i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus_handle, uint16_t address, int xfer_timeout_ms);
  254  
  255  /**
  256   * @brief Execute a series of pre-defined I2C operations.
  257   *
  258   * This function processes a list of I2C operations, such as start, write, read, and stop,
  259   * according to the user-defined `i2c_operation_job_t` array. It performs these operations
  260   * sequentially on the specified I2C master device.
  261   *
  262   * @param[in] i2c_dev           Handle to the I2C master device.
  263   * @param[in] i2c_operation     Pointer to an array of user-defined I2C operation jobs.
  264   *                              Each job specifies a command and associated parameters.
  265   * @param[in] operation_list_num The number of operations in the `i2c_operation` array.
  266   * @param[in] xfer_timeout_ms   Timeout for the transaction, in milliseconds.
  267   *
  268   * @return
  269   *  - ESP_OK: Transaction completed successfully.
  270   *  - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: One or more arguments are invalid.
  271   *  - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Transaction timed out.
  272   *  - ESP_FAIL: Other error during transaction.
  273   *
  274   * @note The `ack_value` field in the READ operation must be set to `I2C_NACK_VAL` if the next
  275   *       operation is a STOP command.
  276   */
  277  esp_err_t i2c_master_execute_defined_operations(i2c_master_dev_handle_t i2c_dev, i2c_operation_job_t *i2c_operation, size_t operation_list_num, int xfer_timeout_ms);
  278

  280   * @brief Register I2C transaction callbacks for a master device
  281   *
  282   * @note User can deregister a previously registered callback by calling this function and setting the callback member in the `cbs` structure to NULL.

🔧 create_textdoc 
{
  "path": "\\\\?\\C:\\Users\\TALWA\\OneDrive\\Desktop\\firmgen\\projects\\esp32_test\\my_firmware\\blinky_button\\firmware\\services\\i2c_scanner_service.c",
  "content": "#include \"i2c_scanner_service.h\"\n#include \"app_config.h\"\n#include \"logger.h\"\n#include \"driver/i2c_master.h\"\n#include \"esp_err.h\"\n\nstatic const char *TAG = \"i2c_scan\";\n\nint i2c_scanner_service_start(void)\n{\n    const i2c_master_bus_config_t bus_config = {\n        .i2c_port = I2C_NUM_0,\n        .sda_io_num = APP_I2C_SDA_GPIO,\n        .scl_io_num = APP_I2C_SCL_GPIO,\n        .clk_source = I2C_CLK_SRC_DEFAULT,\n        .glitch_ignore_cnt = 7,\n        .flags.enable_internal_pullup = true,\n    };\n    i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus = NULL;\n    esp_err_t err = i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_config, &bus);\n    if (err != ESP_OK) {\n        ESP_LOGE(TAG, \"I2C bus initialization failed: %s\", esp_err_to_name(err));\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    bool found_mpu = false;\n    ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"scanning I2C bus SDA=%d SCL=%d at %d Hz\", APP_I2C_SDA_GPIO, APP_I2C_SCL_GPIO, APP_I2C_FREQUENCY_HZ);\n    for (uint8_t address = 0x03; address <= 0x77; ++address) {\n        err = i2c_master_probe(bus, address, APP_I2C_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS);\n        if (err == ESP_OK) {\n            ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"device found at 0x%02X\", address);\n            if (address == 0x68 || address == 0x69) {\n                found_mpu = true;\n                ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"MPU6050-compatible address detected: 0x%02X\", address);\n            }\n        } else if (err == ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT) {\n            ESP_LOGW(TAG, \"probe timeout at 0x%02X; check pull-ups and wiring\", address);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (found_mpu) {\n        ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"MPU6050 scanner test passed\");\n    } else {\n        ESP_LOGW(TAG, \"no MPU6050 found at 0x68 or 0x69\");\n    }\n    i2c_del_master_bus(bus);\n    ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"I2C scan complete\");\n    return found_mpu ? 0 : 1;\n}\n"
}

\"driver/i2c_master.h\"\n#include \"esp_err.h\"\n#include <stdint.h>\n#include <stdbool.h>\n\nstatic const char *TAG = \"mpu6050\";\nstatic i2c_master_dev_handle_t s_device;\n\n#define 0x3B\n#define MPU6050_DATA_LENGTH       14\n#define MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS        100\n\nstatic esp_err_t register_read(uint8_t reg, uint8_t *data, size_t length)\n{\n    return i2c_master_transmit_receive(s_device, &reg, 1, data, length, MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS);\n}\n\nstatic esp_err_t register_write(uint8_t reg, uint8_t value)\n{\n    const uint8_t buffer[2] = {reg, value};\n    return i2c_master_transmit(s_device, buffer, sizeof(buffer), MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS);\n}\n\nstatic int16_t read_i16(const uint8_t *data)\n{\n    return (int16_t)(((uint16_t)data[0] << 8) | data[1]);\n}\n\nstatic void mpu6050_task(void *arg)\n{\n    (void)arg;\n    uint8_t data[MPU6050_DATA_LENGTH];\n\n    for (;;) {\n        esp_err_t err = register_read(MPU6050_REG_ACCEL_XOUT_H, data, sizeof(data));\n        if (err == ESP_OK) {\n            const int16_t accel_x = read_i16(&data[0]);\n            const int16_t accel_y = read_i16(&data[2]);\n            const int16_t accel_z = read_i16(&data[4]);\n            const int16_t temperature_raw = read_i16(&data[6]);\n            const int16_t gyro_x = read_i16(&data[8]);\n            const int16_t gyro_y = read_i16(&data[10]);\n            const int16_t gyro_z = read_i16(&data[12]);\n\n            ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"accel=(%.3f, %.3f, %.3f) g temp=%.2f C gyro=(%.2f, %.2f, %.2f) dps\",\n                     (double)accel_x / 16384.0,\n                     (double)accel_y / 16384.0,\n                     (double)accel_z / 16384.0,\n                     (double)temperature_raw / 340.0 + 36.53,\n                     (double)gyro_x / 131.0,\n                     (double)gyro_y / 131.0,\n                     (double)gyro_z / 131.0);\n        } else {\n            ESP_LOGW(TAG, \"sensor read failed: %s\", 📝 Code changes (1 chunk)
📄 edit: \\?\C:\Users\TALWA\OneDrive\Desktop\firmgen\projects\esp32_test\my_firmware\blinky_button\firmware\services\mpu6050_service.c (L1–1)
+ #include "mpu6050_service.h"
+ #include "app_config.h"
+ #include "logger.h"
+ #include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
+ #include "freertos/task.h"
+ #include "driver/i2c_master.h"
+ #include "esp_err.h"
+ #include <stdint.h>
+ #include <stdbool.h>
+ static const char *TAG = "mpu6050";
+ static i2c_master_dev_handle_t s_device;
+ #define MPU6050_ADDR_PRIMARY 0x68
+ #define MPU6050_ADDR_SECONDARY 0x69
+ #define MPU6050_REG_WHO_AM_I 0x75
+ #define MPU6050_REG_PWR_MGMT_1 0x6B
+ #define MPU6050_REG_ACCEL_XOUT_H 0x3B
+ #define MPU6050_DATA_LENGTH 14
+ #define MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS 100
+ static esp_err_t register_read(uint8_t reg, uint8_t *data, size_t length)
+ {
+  return i2c_master_transmit_receive(s_device, &reg, 1, data, length, MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS);
+ }
+ static esp_err_t register_write(uint8_t reg, uint8_t value)
+ {
+  const uint8_t buffer[2] = {reg, value};
+  return i2c_master_transmit(s_device, buffer, sizeof(buffer), MPU6050_TIMEOUT_MS);
+ }
+ static int16_t read_i16(const uint8_t *data)
+ {
+  return (int16_t)(((uint16_t)data[0] << 8) | data[1]);
+ }
+ static void mpu6050_task(void *arg)
+ {
+  (void)arg;
+  uint8_t data[MPU6050_DATA_LENGTH];
+  for (;;) {
+  esp_err_t err = register_read(MPU6050_REG_ACCEL_XOUT_H, data, sizeof(data));
+  if (err == ESP_OK) {
+  const int16_t accel_x = read_i16(&data[0]);
+  const int16_t accel_y = read_i16(&data[2]);
+  const int16_t accel_z = read_i16(&data[4]);
+  const int16_t temperature_raw = read_i16(&data[6]);
+  const int16_t gyro_x = read_i16(&data[8]);
+  const int16_t gyro_y = read_i16(&data[10]);
+  const int16_t gyro_z = read_i16(&data[12]);
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "accel=(%.3f, %.3f, %.3f) g temp=%.2f C gyro=(%.2f, %.2f, %.2f) dps",
+  (double)accel_x / 16384.0,
+  (double)accel_y / 16384.0,
+  (double)accel_z / 16384.0,
+  (double)temperature_raw / 340.0 + 36.53,
+  (double)gyro_x / 131.0,
+  (double)gyro_y / 131.0,
+  (double)gyro_z / 131.0);
+  } else {
+  ESP_LOGW(TAG, "sensor read failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  }
+  vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(100));
+  }
+ }
+ int mpu6050_service_start(void)
+ {
+  const i2c_master_bus_config_t bus_config = {
+  .i2c_port = I2C_NUM_0,
+  .sda_io_num = APP_I2C_SDA_GPIO,
+  .scl_io_num = APP_I2C_SCL_GPIO,
+  .clk_source = I2C_CLK_SRC_DEFAULT,
+  .glitch_ignore_cnt = 7,
+  .flags.enable_internal_pullup = true,
+  };
+  i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus = NULL;
+  esp_err_t err = i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_config, &bus);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "I2C bus initialization failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  return -1;
+  }
+  uint16_t address = 0;
+  if (i2c_master_probe(bus, MPU6050_ADDR_PRIMARY, APP_I2C_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS) == ESP_OK) {
+  address = MPU6050_ADDR_PRIMARY;
+  } else if (i2c_master_probe(bus, MPU6050_ADDR_SECONDARY, APP_I2C_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS) == ESP_OK) {
+  address = MPU6050_ADDR_SECONDARY;
+  }
+  if (address == 0) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "MPU6050 not found at 0x68 or 0x69");
+  i2c_del_master_bus(bus);
+  return -1;
+  }
+  const i2c_device_config_t device_config = {
+  .dev_addr_length = I2C_ADDR_BIT_LEN_7,
+  .device_address = address,
+  .scl_speed_hz = APP_I2C_FREQUENCY_HZ,
+  };
+  err = i2c_master_bus_add_device(bus, &device_config, &s_device);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "MPU6050 device setup failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  i2c_del_master_bus(bus);
+  return -1;
+  }
+  uint8_t who_am_i = 0;
+  err = register_read(MPU6050_REG_WHO_AM_I, &who_am_i, 1);
+  if (err != ESP_OK || who_am_i != 0x68) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "WHO_AM_I check failed: err=%s value=0x%02X", esp_err_to_name(err), who_am_i);
+  i2c_master_bus_rm_device(s_device);
+  i2c_del_master_bus(bus);
+  s_device = NULL;
+  return -1;
+  }
+  err = register_write(MPU6050_REG_PWR_MGMT_1, 0x00);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "failed to wake MPU6050: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
+  i2c_master_bus_rm_device(s_device);
+  i2c_del_master_bus(bus);
+  s_device = NULL;
+  return -1;
+  }
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MPU6050 ready at 0x%02X; polling at 10 Hz", address);
+  if (xTaskCreate(mpu6050_task, "mpu6050_task", 4096, NULL, 4, NULL) != pdPASS) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "failed to create MPU6050 task");
+  i2c_master_bus_rm_device(s_device);
+  i2c_del_master_bus(bus);
+  s_device = NULL;
+  return -1;
+  }
+  return 0;
+ }

🔧 create_textdoc 
{
  "path": "\\\\?\\C:\\Users\\TALWA\\OneDrive\\Desktop\\firmgen\\projects\\esp32_test\\my_firmware\\blinky_button\\firmware\\docs\\overview.md",
  "content": "# ESP32-C3 Blinky Button + MPU6050 Firmware\n\nThis firmware blinks the ESP32-C3 DevKitM-1 onboard RGB LED blue and logs motion data from an MPU6050 over I2C. It starts blinking at 1 Hz and increases the frequency by 1 Hz for each debounced BOOT-button press, up to 10 Hz. The MPU6050 is initialized at address 0x68 and polled at 10 Hz.\n\n## Hardware map and wiring\n\n- Onboard RGB LED data: GPIO8\n- Onboard BOOT button: GPIO9, active-low with internal pull-up\n- MPU6050 3.3V/VCC: ESP32-C3 3V3\n- MPU6050 GND: ESP32-C3 GND\n- MPU6050 SDA: ESP32-C3 GPIO4\n- MPU6050 SCL: ESP32-C3 GPIO5\n- MPU6050 address detected by scanner: 0x68\n- An additional device responded at 0x77; it is ignored by the MPU6050 service.\n\nThe LED uses blue brightness 64/255 and a 50% duty cycle. The I2C bus runs at 100 kHz with internal pull-ups enabled; external pull-ups may still be needed depending on the breakout board.\n\n## Sensor logging\n\nThe firmware reads the MPU6050 register block from 0x3B through 0x48 every 100 ms. It logs acceleration in g using the ±2 g scale, temperature in degrees Celsius, and angular rate in degrees per second using the ±250 dps scale. WHO_AM_I is checked during initialization and the device is woken through PWR_MGMT_1.\n\n## Build and flash\n\nThe project targets ESP32-C3 and uses ESP-IDF with the managed `espressif/led_strip` component and the modern `driver/i2c_master.h` API. Build and flash using the ESP-IDF project tooling, then monitor at 115200 baud. Startup, sensor, and accepted button-press messages are logged with the `app`, `mpu6050`, and `blinky` tags.\n\n## Modules\n\n- `firmware/app/app.c`: application startup orchestration.\n- `firmware/services/blinky_button_service.c`: LED initialization, debounced BOOT-button polling, press queue, and blink-frequency state machine.\n- `firmware/services/mpu6050_service.c`: I2C bus/device initialization, WHO_AM_I validation, wake-up, polling, conversion, and logging.\n- `firmware/services/i2c_scanner_service.c`: temporary hardware-validation scanner used to detect 0x68 and 0x77.\n- `firmware/configs/app_config.h`: GPIO assignments and application constants.\n- `firmware/utils/logger/logger.h`: ESP-IDF logging include wrapper.\n"
}

  "content": "# blinky_button\n\nESP32-C3 firmware combining a blue onboard LED blink controller, BOOT-button frequency control, and MPU6050 motion-sensor logging over I2C.\n\n## Hardware\n\n- LED: onboard RGB LED on GPIO8\n- BOOT button: GPIO9, active-low\n- MPU6050 power: 3.3V/VCC to ESP32-C3 3V3\n- MPU6050 ground: GND to ESP32-C3 GND\n- MPU6050 SDA: GPIO4\n- MPU6050 SCL: GPIO5\n- Detected MPU6050 address: 0x68\n\nThe I2C scanner also detected a device at 0x77; the MPU6050 service addresses only 0x68 or 0x69.\n\n## Runtime behavior\n\nThe LED starts blinking blue at 1 Hz. Each debounced BOOT-button press increases the frequency by 1 Hz, capped at 10 Hz. The MPU6050 is read every 100 ms and logs acceleration, temperature, and gyroscope values at 115200 baud. The final firmware verified WHO_AM_I and continued reading the sensor without reported I2C errors during the monitor capture.\n\n## Layout\n\n- `app/` — application entry/orchestration (`app_start()` in `app.c`); includes service headers only.\n- `services/` — blinky/button and MPU6050 services.\n- `configs/` — compile-time settings in `app_config.h`.\n- `utils/` — logging and helpers.\n- `docs/` — project documentation and generated API report.\n\nThe ESP-IDF entry point `app_main()` lives once in `../main/entry.c` and calls `app_start()`. The `main/` component is generated build glue; edit the `firmware/` tree for application changes.\n"

📄 edit: \\?\C:\Users\TALWA\OneDrive\Desktop\firmgen\projects\esp32_test\my_firmware\blinky_button\firmware\README.md (L7–7)
+ ## Hardware
+ - LED: onboard RGB LED on GPIO8
+ - BOOT button: GPIO9, active-low
+ - MPU6050 power: 3.3V/VCC to ESP32-C3 3V3
+ - MPU6050 ground: GND to ESP32-C3 GND
+ - MPU6050 SDA: GPIO4
+ - MPU6050 SCL: GPIO5
+ - Detected MPU6050 address: 0x68
+ The I2C scanner also detected a device at 0x77; the MPU6050 service addresses only 0x68 or 0x69.
+ ## Runtime behavior
+ The LED starts blinking blue at 1 Hz. Each debounced BOOT-button press increases the frequency by 1 Hz, capped at 10 Hz. The MPU6050 is read every 100 ms and logs acceleration, temperature, and gyroscope values at 115200 baud. The final firmware verified WHO_AM_I and continued reading the sensor without reported I2C errors during the monitor capture.

+ The LED uses blue brightness 64/255 and a 50% duty cycle. The I2C bus runs at 100 kHz with internal pull-ups enabled; external pull-ups may still be needed depending on the breakout board.
+ ## Sensor logging
+ The firmware reads the MPU6050 register block from 0x3B through 0x48 every 100 ms. It logs acceleration in g using the ±2 g scale, temperature in degrees Celsius, and angular rate in degrees per second using the ±250 dps scale. WHO_AM_I is checked during initialization and the device is woken through PWR_MGMT_1.

Build and flash using the ESP-IDF project tooling, then monitor at 115200 baud. Startup, sensor, and accepted button-press messages are logged with the `app`, `mpu6050`, and `blinky` tags.

#include <string.h>

/* Define GPIO pin for collecting button input. */
#define BUTTON_NODE DT_ALIAS(sw0)
static const struct gpio_dt_spec button = GPIO_DT_SPEC_GET(BUTTON_NODE, gpios);

#define SENSOR_TYPE 0x1E
#define TOTAL_PACKETS 1



#define DEVICE_NAME CONFIG_BT_DEVICE_NAME
#define DEVICE_NAME_LEN (sizeof(DEVICE_NAME) - 1)

struct bt_le_ext_adv *adv;
const struct device *sensor = DEVICE_DT_GET_ANY(st_lis2dh);

float threshold = 7;
static float xval[50] = {0};
static float yval[50] = {0};
static float zval[50] = {0};
static float xavg, yavg, zavg;
int steps = 0, flag = 0;

int err;

struct bt_le_adv_param adv_param = BT_LE_ADV_PARAM_INIT(BT_LE_ADV_OPT_NONE | BT_LE_ADV_OPT_EXT_ADV | BT_LE_ADV_OPT_USE_IDENTITY | BT_LE_ADV_OPT_NO_2M, 0x30, 0x30, NULL);
struct bt_le_ext_adv_start_param ext_adv_param = BT_LE_EXT_ADV_START_PARAM_INIT(0, 2);
static uint8_t mfg_data[6] = {0};
static const struct bt_data ad[] = {
BT_DATA(BT_DATA_MANUFACTURER_DATA, mfg_data, sizeof(mfg_data)),
BT_DATA(BT_DATA_NAME_COMPLETE, DEVICE_NAME, DEVICE_NAME_LEN)
};

/* Define entry point for thread (target function). */
void button_pressed(void *, void *, void *) {

calibrate();
steps = -1;

}

#define MY_STACK_SIZE 1024
#define MY_PRIORITY 5

K_THREAD_STACK_DEFINE(my_stack_area, MY_STACK_SIZE);
static struct k_thread my_thread_data;

static void set_random_static_address(void) {
printk("Starting iBeacon Demo\n");
bt_addr_le_t addr;
err = bt_addr_le_from_str("FE:AB:BB:AF:BA:11", "random", &addr);
if (err) {
printk("Invalid BT address (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}
err = bt_id_create(&addr, NULL);
if (err < 0) {
printk("Creating new ID failed (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}
printk("Created new address\n");
}

void adv_param_init(void) {
err = bt_le_ext_adv_create(&adv_param, NULL, &adv);
if (err) {
printk("Failed to create advertising set (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}
printk("Created extended advertising set\n");
}

void fetch_acc_data(void) {
static float xaccl[1] = {0};
static float yaccl[1] = {0};
static float zaccl[1] = {0};
static float totvect[1] = {0};
static float totave[1] = {0};

mfg_data[0] = 0x59;
mfg_data[1] = 0x00;
mfg_data[2] = 175;


for (int a = 0; a < 1; a++) {
struct sensor_value accel[3];
err = sensor_sample_fetch(sensor);
if (err == 0) {
sensor_channel_get(sensor, SENSOR_CHAN_ACCEL_XYZ, accel);
float accel_value1 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[0]);
float accel_value2 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[1]);
float accel_value3 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[2]);
xaccl[a] = accel_value1;
yaccl[a] = accel_value2;
zaccl[a] = accel_value3;
} else {
printk("ERROR: Accelerometer data update failed: %d\n", err);
}

totvect[a] = sqrt((xaccl[a] - xavg)*(xaccl[a] - xavg) + (yaccl[a] - yavg)*(yaccl[a] - yavg) + (zaccl[a] - zavg)*(zaccl[a] - zavg));
totave[a] = (a == 0) ? totvect[a] : (totvect[a] + totvect[a - 1]) / 2;

if (totave[a] > threshold && flag == 0) {
steps++;
flag = 1;


} else if (totave[a] < threshold && flag == 1) {
flag = 0;
}

if (steps < 0) {
steps = 0;
}

printk("Step detected! Total steps: %d\n", steps);
mfg_data[3] = (steps >> 8) & 0xFF; // High byte
mfg_data[4] = steps & 0xFF; // Low byte
mfg_data[5] = 0x00;

start_adv();
k_sleep(K_MSEC(100));

// k_sleep(K_MSEC(100));
}


}

void calibrate(void) {
float sum = 0, sum1 = 0, sum2 = 0;

for (int i = 0; i < 50; i++) {
struct sensor_value accel[3];
err = sensor_sample_fetch(sensor);
if (err == 0) {
sensor_channel_get(sensor, SENSOR_CHAN_ACCEL_XYZ, accel);
float accel_value1 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[0]);
float accel_value2 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[1]);
float accel_value3 = sensor_value_to_double(&accel[2]);
xval[i] = accel_value1;
yval[i] = accel_value2;
zval[i] = accel_value3;
sum += xval[i];
sum1 += yval[i];
sum2 += zval[i];
k_sleep(K_MSEC(100));
} else {
printk("ERROR: Accelerometer data update failed: %d\n", err);
}
}

xavg = sum / 50.0;
yavg = sum1 / 50.0;
zavg = sum2 / 50.0;

printk("xavg = %f, yavg = %f, zavg = %f\n", xavg, yavg, zavg);
}

void start_adv(void) {
err = bt_le_ext_adv_set_data(adv, ad, ARRAY_SIZE(ad), NULL, 0);
if (err) {
printk("Failed (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}
err = bt_le_ext_adv_start(adv, &ext_adv_param);
if (err) {
printk("Failed to start extended advertising (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}

k_msleep(100);
bt_le_ext_adv_stop(adv);
// fetch_acc_data();
}

void main(void) {
nrf_gpio_cfg_output(DT_GPIO_PIN(DT_NODELABEL(led3), gpios));
nrf_gpio_pin_set(DT_GPIO_PIN(DT_NODELABEL(led3), gpios));
printk("Starting STEPS Node\n");

set_random_static_address();

err = bt_enable(NULL);
if (err) {
printk("Bluetooth init failed (err %d)\n", err);
return;
}
adv_param_init();

/* Setup GPIO for button */
if (!gpio_is_ready_dt(&button)) {
printk("Button device not ready\n");
return;
}
gpio_pin_configure_dt(&button, GPIO_INPUT);

while (1) {
int val = gpio_pin_get_dt(&button);
if (val == 1) {
k_thread_create(&my_thread_data, my_stack_area,
K_THREAD_STACK_SIZEOF(my_stack_area),
button_pressed, NULL, NULL, NULL,
MY_PRIORITY, 0, K_NO_WAIT);
k_thread_join(&my_thread_data, K_FOREVER);
}else {
fetch_acc_data();
}
// k_msleep(100);
}
}

✅ Tool result 
User answered the clarifying questions:
- BLE output: No BLE
- Reset behavior: use this : The key distinction: BOOT button ≠ hardware reset
Your worry is valid for the EN/RST button (the real reset pin on most ESP32 dev boards) — that one power-cycles the whole chip, so yes, it would kill and re-establish Wi-Fi/MQTT every time. Don't use that one for step resets.

Instead, use the BOOT button (GPIO0) read purely as a software GPIO input while the firmware is already running (not for entering flash mode — just reading its digital state in your normal loop). Read this way, pressing it does nothing to Wi-Fi or MQTT by itself — it only does whatever your code chooses to do in response, which we control. So:

Proposed behavior
BOOT button (GPIO0, debounced):

Short press → reset the step counter to 0 only. Wi-Fi stays connected, MQTT stays connected, nothing reconnects. Just zeroes a variable and logs it.
Long press (hold ~2s) → trigger recalibration — recompute the resting-baseline average (the xavg/yavg/zavg calibration step from your reference algorithm) in case the device got remounted or reoriented. Genuinely useful to have a physical trigger for this rather than only doing it once at boot.
Onboard LED:

Slow blink while joining Wi-Fi/MQTT (mirrors what's currently only visible in serial logs).
Solid on once connected and actively counting steps.
Quick flash (~60ms) on every step detected — this matters because ThingSpeak's publish cadence is locked to 15s, so without this you'd have no real-time feedback that the device is even sensing motion between publishes.
Fast rapid blink during the ~5s recalibration window, so you know to hold the device still.
Distinct double-blink pattern if Wi-Fi/MQTT ever drops, so you can tell from across the room without opening a serial monitor.
This keeps reset, calibration, and connection status all readable from one LED + one button, with zero risk of an accidental network drop when you just want to zero the count
- ThingSpeak field: Field 1

For anything beyond a trivial single-file change, call `strategic_planning` now — it finalizes the plan and creates the task list. Only after the task list exists may you start coding.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Inspect the existing `my_firmware` project before changing files. Locate the application entry point, Wi-Fi station manager, MQTT/ThingSpeak client, source-registration mechanism, ESP-IDF version, and the existing credentials in `main/app_config.h`.
2. Resolve the implementation boundary from that inspection: reuse the existing Wi-Fi/MQTT client and connection callbacks rather than creating a second network client. BLE will not be added; ThingSpeak MQTT will run over the existing Wi-Fi station connection.
3. Add non-secret configuration for the MPU6050, step detector, button, LED, and ThingSpeak publication. Defaults are I2C on SDA GPIO4/SCL GPIO5 at 100 kHz, probe addresses 0x68 and 0x69, 50 Hz accelerometer sampling, 5-second stationary calibration, 0.20 g detection threshold with 0.10 g release hysteresis, 300 ms minimum step interval, volatile uint32 step count, ThingSpeak Field 1, and a 15-second minimum publish interval.
4. Implement an MPU6050 service using the I2C driver API supported by `my_firmware`. Probe 0x68 and 0x69, validate WHO_AM_I, wake the device, read accelerometer data, convert raw values to g, and report recoverable I2C errors without taking down Wi-Fi or the application.
5. Implement a step-counter service based on the supplied algorithm but with g-based thresholds and production safeguards. Average calibrated X/Y/Z values during startup and recalibration, calculate the Euclidean deviation from that baseline, accept a step only on a threshold crossing, require the signal to fall below the release threshold before another step, enforce the refractory interval, and emit a bounded step event containing the newest cumulative count.
6. Implement the board-specific BOOT-button control using GPIO9, which is the BOOT button defined for this ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1. Treat it as active-low with an internal pull-up and approximately 50 ms debounce. A short press resets only the software count to zero; it must not disconnect or restart Wi-Fi/MQTT. A continuous hold of approximately 2 seconds starts a 5-second recalibration. Require release before accepting another command and ignore an initially held button until it has been released. Do not use GPIO0 or the EN/RST hardware reset circuit for this function.
7. Implement a nonblocking LED status service for the addressable RGB LED on GPIO8 using `led_strip`. Use blue output with moderate brightness: slow blinking while Wi-Fi/MQTT is connecting, solid blue while connected and counting, a 60 ms step flash for every accepted step, a fast blink during recalibration, and a distinct double-blink pattern after a previously connected network session drops. Give recalibration and step flashes priority over the normal connection pattern, then return to the current connection state.
8. Connect accepted step and reset events to the existing MQTT integration through a bounded adapter. The logical count must update even when the network is unavailable. While disconnected or rate-limited, retain only the newest pending count; after reconnection and when the 15-second ThingSpeak interval permits, publish that count to Field 1 using the existing ThingSpeak topic and authentication flow. Do not log credentials, API keys, or full MQTT secrets.
9. Register the new MPU6050, step-counter, button, and LED sources through the existing build/source-registration system. Update application startup so services initialize exactly once, failed resource allocation prevents partially initialized tasks from starting, sensor failures remain non-fatal, and the existing Wi-Fi/MQTT initialization is preserved.
10. Build the complete firmware and resolve component, include, driver-version, linker, and source-registration errors using the APIs discovered during inspection. Confirm that no restricted GPIO11–17 pins are assigned and that the existing credentials remain unchanged.
11. After the build succeeds, verify the ESP32-C3 serial port rather than assuming a port number. If the port is unavailable or busy, close monitors and terminals, reconnect the board, and confirm the port belongs to this device before flashing.
12. Flash and monitor the device. Verify MPU6050 detection and WHO_AM_I, startup calibration, stable stationary readings, changing readings during movement, one logical count increment per step, short-press reset without MQTT disconnect, long-press recalibration, LED status patterns, Wi-Fi/MQTT reconnect behavior, and latest-count delivery after a temporary network outage. Confirm ThingSpeak Field 1 receives updates no faster than every 15 seconds.
13. Document the final wiring and behavior: MPU6050 3.3V/GND/SDA GPIO4/SCL GPIO5, detected address, calibration and threshold units, volatile reset semantics, Field 1 publication cadence, Wi-Fi/MQTT status handling, GPIO8 LED patterns, and GPIO9 BOOT-button controls.

Defaults: Wi-Fi station mode and the existing MQTT/ThingSpeak implementation are retained; no BLE, SoftAP, HTTP server, interrupt pin, NVS count persistence, or MPU6050 INT wire is added. The software BOOT-button reset changes only the volatile count. A physical EN/RST reset will naturally reboot the entire ESP32 and therefore restart Wi-Fi/MQTT, but it is not used as the count-reset control.

**Alternatives:** If the existing MQTT code has no publish callback or event interface, add a small adapter around its current client rather than replacing the working Wi-Fi/MQTT manager. If ThingSpeak rejects per-step traffic, retain the newest cumulative count and publish it at the next permitted interval. If the 0.20 g detector threshold is too sensitive or insensitive for the mounting position, log calibrated vector deviation and tune the threshold and hysteresis without changing the event or MQTT architecture. If MPU6050 reads fail, keep the network and LED services alive, retry with bounded backoff, and check power, common ground, SDA/SCL wiring, pull-ups, and the selected 0x68/0x69 address. If the addressable LED dependency or API differs, use the installed version’s `led_strip` API and retain GPIO8 rather than replacing the RGB LED with a restricted pin.

AI

Plan:

  • Reuse the existing Wi‑Fi/MQTT/ThingSpeak implementation in my_firmware; no BLE will be added.
  • Integrate the MPU6050 over I²C:

- SDA: GPIO4 - SCL: GPIO5 - Address: detect 0x68/0x69 - Accelerometer polling at 50 Hz

  • Implement the supplied step algorithm with:

- 5-second stationary calibration - Baseline X/Y/Z averaging - Vector-deviation step detection - Threshold and release hysteresis to prevent duplicate counts - Minimum interval between steps

  • Use the ESP32-C3 board’s BOOT button on GPIO9 as a software input:

- Short press: reset the volatile count only - Hold for about 2 seconds: start recalibration - No Wi‑Fi or MQTT restart from either action

  • Use the onboard RGB LED on GPIO8 for status:

- Slow blink: connecting - Solid blue: connected and counting - Brief flash: step detected - Fast blink: recalibration - Double blink: connection lost

  • Publish the cumulative count to ThingSpeak Field 1, at most once every 15 seconds, retaining the newest count during outages.
  • Keep sensor/network failures non-fatal and preserve the existing MQTT credentials without logging secrets.
  • Build, flash, monitor, and document the completed firmware.

Important distinction: pressing the software BOOT button will only reset the count. Pressing the physical EN/RST reset will reboot the ESP32 and naturally restart Wi‑Fi/MQTT, but it will not be used as the step-reset control.

# Current task list (plan v5)
- t1 (completed): Inspect the existing `my_firmware` project structure, `main/app_config.h`, application entry point, Wi-Fi station code, MQTT/ThingSpeak code, ESP-IDF version, and source-registration/build files. Record the existing initialization, publish, and connection-state APIs without modifying credentials.
- t2 (completed): 🧠 Decide: Existing network and driver integration boundary [DEFERRED — decide: Which existing application function or service owns Wi-Fi/MQTT connection state and ThingSpeak publishing, and does the project use the modern ESP-IDF I2C master API or the legacy I2C driver?]
- t3 (completed): Update `main/app_config.h` with non-secret MPU6050, step detector, button, LED, and ThingSpeak settings: GPIO4/GPIO5 I2C, 100 kHz bus speed, 0x68/0x69 probing, 50 Hz sampling, 5-second calibration, 0.20 g threshold, 0.10 g release hysteresis, 300 ms refractory interval, 50 ms button debounce, 2-second long-press duration, 5-second recalibration duration, GPIO9 BOOT input, GPIO8 LED output, Field 1, and a 15-second MQTT publication interval. Preserve all existing credentials.
- t4 (completed): Create the MPU6050 service in the project’s existing source layout. Initialize one I2C master bus on GPIO4/GPIO5 using the API selected in t2, probe 0x68 and 0x69, validate WHO_AM_I, wake the sensor through PWR_MGMT_1, read the accelerometer register block, convert signed raw values to g, and expose bounded polling/error status to the step detector.
- t5 (completed): Create the step-counter service. Perform a stationary five-second baseline calibration, compute the vector deviation from the calibrated X/Y/Z average, apply threshold crossing plus release hysteresis, enforce the minimum step interval, maintain a volatile uint32 cumulative count, and emit bounded accepted-step events containing the newest count.
- t8 (completed): Add a step-event/MQTT adapter at the existing network integration boundary. Keep the newest pending cumulative count when disconnected or rate-limited, publish it to ThingSpeak Field 1 through the existing MQTT client at most once per 15 seconds, deliver reset counts as normal latest-count updates, and map Wi-Fi/MQTT state changes to the LED service.

The existing plan already covers this — no revision was needed. Continue implementing against it: gather any missing evidence with a direct tool (example_show, board_pins, cat, mcu_docs), then edit the sources and build. Build errors and missing symbols are fixed in code, not by re-planning.

                    INCLUDE_DIRS ".")


// ===== main/Kconfig.projbuild =====
menu "Example Configuration"

    config BROKER_URL
        string "Broker URL"
        default "mqtt://mqtt.eclipseprojects.io"
        help
            URL of the broker to connect to

    config BROKER_URL_FROM_STDIN
        bool
        default y if BROKER_URL = "FROM_STDIN"

// ===== main/app_main.c =====
/* MQTT (over TCP) Example

   This example code is in the Public Domain (or CC0 licensed, at your option.)

   Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, this
   software is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR
   CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
*/

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <inttypes.h>
#include "esp_system.h"
#include "nvs_flash.h"
#include "esp_event.h"
#include "esp_netif.h"
#include "protocol_examples_common.h"

#include "esp_log.h"
#include "mqtt_client.h"

static const char *TAG = "mqtt_example";


static void log_error_if_nonzero(const char *message, int error_code)
{
    if (error_code != 0) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Last error %s: 0x%x", message, error_code);
    }
}

/*
 * @brief Event handler registered to receive MQTT events
 *
 *  This function is called by the MQTT client event loop.
 *
 * @param handler_args user data registered to the event.
 * @param base Event base for the handler(always MQTT Base in this example).
 * @param event_id The id for the received event.
 * @param event_data The data for the event, esp_mqtt_event_handle_t.
 */
static void mqtt_event_handler(void *handler_args, esp_event_base_t base, int32_t event_id, void *event_data)
{
    ESP_LOGD(TAG, "Event dispatched from event loop base=%s, event_id=%" PRIi32 "", base, event_id);
    esp_mqtt_event_handle_t event = event_data;
    esp_mqtt_client_handle_t client = event->client;
    int msg_id;
    switch ((esp_mqtt_event_id_t)event_id) {
    case MQTT_EVENT_CONNECTED:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_CONNECTED");
        msg_id = esp_mqtt_client_publish(client, "/topic/qos1", "data_3", 0, 1, 0);
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "sent publish successful, msg_id=%d", msg_id);

        msg_id = esp_mqtt_client_subscribe(client, "/topic/qos0", 0);
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "sent subscribe successful, msg_id=%d", msg_id);

        msg_id = esp_mqtt_client_subscribe(client, "/topic/qos1", 1);
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "sent subscribe successful, msg_id=%d", msg_id);

        msg_id = esp_mqtt_client_unsubscribe(client, "/topic/qos1");
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "sent unsubscribe successful, msg_id=%d", msg_id);
        break;
    case MQTT_EVENT_DISCONNECTED:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_DISCONNECTED");
        break;

    case MQTT_EVENT_SUBSCRIBED:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_SUBSCRIBED, msg_id=%d", event->msg_id);
        msg_id = esp_mqtt_client_publish(client, "/topic/qos0", "data", 0, 0, 0);
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "sent publish successful, msg_id=%d", msg_id);
        break;
    case MQTT_EVENT_UNSUBSCRIBED:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_UNSUBSCRIBED, msg_id=%d", event->msg_id);
        break;
    case MQTT_EVENT_PUBLISHED:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_PUBLISHED, msg_id=%d", event->msg_id);
        break;
    case MQTT_EVENT_DATA:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_DATA");
        printf("TOPIC=%.*s\r\n", event->topic_len, event->topic);
        printf("DATA=%.*s\r\n", event->data_len, event->data);
        break;
    case MQTT_EVENT_ERROR:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MQTT_EVENT_ERROR");
        if (event->error_handle->error_type == MQTT_ERROR_TYPE_TCP_TRANSPORT) {
            log_error_if_nonzero("reported from esp-tls", event->error_handle->esp_tls_last_esp_err);
            log_error_if_nonzero("reported from tls stack", event->error_handle->esp_tls_stack_err);
            log_error_if_nonzero("captured as transport's socket errno",  event->error_handle->esp_transport_sock_errno);
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Last errno string (%s)", strerror(event->error_handle->esp_transport_sock_errno));

        }
        break;
    default:
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Other event id:%d", event->event_id);
        break;
    }
}

static void mqtt_app_start(void)
{
    esp_mqtt_client_config_t mqtt_cfg = {
        .broker.address.uri = CONFIG_BROKER_URL,
    };
#if CONFIG_BROKER_URL_FROM_STDIN
    char line[128];

    if (strcmp(mqtt_cfg.broker.address.uri, "FROM_STDIN") == 0) {
        int count = 0;
        printf("Please enter url of mqtt broker\n");
        while (count < 128) {
            int c = fgetc(stdin);
            if (c == '\n') {
                line[count] = '\0';
                break;
            } else if (c > 0 && c < 127) {
                line[count] = c;
                ++count;
            }
            vTaskDelay(10 / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
        }
        mqtt_cfg.broker.address.uri = line;
        printf("Broker url: %s\n", line);
    } else {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Configuration mismatch: wrong broker url");
        abort();
    }
#endif /* CONFIG_BROKER_URL_FROM_STDIN */

    esp_mqtt_client_handle_t client = esp_mqtt_client_init(&mqtt_cfg);
    /* The last argument may be used to pass data to the event handler, in this example mqtt_event_handler */
    esp_mqtt_client_register_event(client, ESP_EVENT_ANY_ID, mqtt_event_handler, NULL);
    esp_mqtt_client_start(client);
}

void app_main(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "[APP] Startup..");
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "[APP] Free memory: %" PRIu32 " bytes", esp_get_free_heap_size());
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "[APP] IDF version: %s", esp_get_idf_version());

    esp_log_level_set("*", ESP_LOG_INFO);
    esp_log_level_set("mqtt_client", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);
    esp_log_level_set("mqtt_example", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);
    esp_log_level_set("transport_base", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);
    esp_log_level_set("esp-tls", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);
    esp_log_level_set("transport", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);
    esp_log_level_set("outbox", ESP_LOG_VERBOSE);

    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(nvs_flash_init());
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_netif_init());
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_loop_create_default());

    /* This helper function configures Wi-Fi or Ethernet, as selected in menuconfig.
     * Read "Establishing Wi-Fi or Ethernet Connection" section in
     * examples/protocols/README.md for more information about this function.
     */
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(example_connect());

    mqtt_app_start();
}

      - if: "target in [esp32p4, esp32h2]"
